{"candidates": ["F90.9"], "note": "Patient shows lifelong history of inattention and hyperactivity-impulsivity symptoms (out of seat, blurting, fidgeting since elementary school). Since then, it's just been a constant struggle to keep it together.\n\nDoctor: When your company made those big changes two years ago, did your attention and focus problems get worse right around that time, or had they already been getting gradually worse before that happened?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. Um... it's kind of hard to say. I just know that right around the time the company changed our schedules and everything, that's when it became really obvious and started actually impacting my work. If it was gradually getting worse before that, I didn't really notice it.\n\nDoctor: Around the time these work changes happened two years ago, did you start experiencing any feelings of worry, anxiety, or nervousness—either about work specifically or more generally about things in your life?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's actually gotten to the point where people have started joking that they can hear me before they even see me.\n\nDoctor: Going back to when you were in elementary school, were you also fidgeting, bouncing your leg, and feeling restless the way you do now—or is that something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it was definitely happening back then too. Um, even in elementary school, my teachers actually had to move my desk because I was always getting out of my seat. I'd be blurting out answers or just bothering my classmates before I even realized I was doing it. I haven't really noticed it being a problem outside of my job. Like, my relationships and managing the house seem fine. And driving is fine too. I mean, I guess I might still be a little fidgety at home sometimes, but it doesn't cause any real issues with my friends or anything like that. It really just seems to be a problem when I'm trying to get through my editing or sitting in those long meetings.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something at home that requires the same kind of sustained attention and detail work as your editing—like maybe following a complex recipe, assembling something, or doing detailed personal projects—do you find yourself making careless mistakes or having trouble focusing the way you do at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed that. If I'm doing something around the house, like cooking or putting something together, I don't really make careless mistakes. It's... I don't know, I just don't have that same trouble focusing when it's my own stuff at home.
